\noindent[\textbf{41:48}]
they shall say, 'We do own to thee there is no witness amongst us!' and that on which
they used to call before shall stray away from them, and they shall think there is no escape for
them.

\noindent[\textbf{41:49}]
Man is never tired of praying for good, but if evil touch him, then he is despairing and
hopeless.

\noindent[\textbf{41:50}]
But if we make him taste mercy from us after distress has touched him he will surely say,
'This is for me, and I do not think the Hour is imminent; and if I be brought back to my Lord,
verily, I shall surely have good with Him;' but we will inform those who misbelieve of what they
have done, and we will surely make them taste wretched torment.

\noindent[\textbf{41:51}]
And when we have been gracious to man, he turns away and goes aside; but when evil
touches him he is one of copious prayer.

\noindent[\textbf{41:52}]
Say, 'Let us see now! if it be from God and ye disbelieve in it, who is more in error than
he who is in a remote schism?'

\noindent[\textbf{41:53}]
We will show them our signs in the regions and in themselves, until it is plain to them
that it is the truth. Is it not enough for thy Lord that He is witness over all?

\noindent[\textbf{41:54}]
Ay, verily, they are in doubt about the meeting of their Lord! Ay, verily, He encompasses
all!

\section{Surah 68: The Chapter of the Pen (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{68:1}]
NUN. By the pen, and what they write,

\noindent[\textbf{68:2}]
thou art not, by God's grace, mad!

\noindent[\textbf{68:3}]
and, verily, thine is a hire that is not grudged!

\noindent[\textbf{68:4}]
and, verily, thou art of a grand nature!

\noindent[\textbf{68:5}]
But thou shalt see and they shall see

\noindent[\textbf{68:6}]
which of you is the infatuated.

\noindent[\textbf{68:7}]
Verily, thy Lord He knows best who errs from His way; and He knows best those who
are guided.

\noindent[\textbf{68:8}]
Then obey not those who call thee liar;

\noindent[\textbf{68:9}]
they would fain that thou shouldst be smooth with them, then would they be smooth with
thee!

\noindent[\textbf{68:10}]
And obey not any mean swearer,

\noindent[\textbf{68:11}]
a back-biter, a walker about with slander;

\noindent[\textbf{68:12}]
a forbidder of good, a transgressor, a sinner;

\noindent[\textbf{68:13}]
rude, and base-born too;

\noindent[\textbf{68:14}]
though he have wealth and sons!

\noindent[\textbf{68:15}]
When our signs are recited to him he says, 'Old folks' tales!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:16}]
We will brand him on the snout!

\noindent[\textbf{68:17}]
Verily, we have tried them as we tried the fellows of the garden when they swore, 'We
will cut its fruit at morn!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:18}]
But they made not the exception;

\noindent[\textbf{68:19}]
and there came round about it an encompassing calamity from thy Lord the while they
slept;

\noindent[\textbf{68:20}]
and on the morrow it was as one the fruit of which is cut.

\noindent[\textbf{68:21}]
And they cried to each other in the morning,

\noindent[\textbf{68:22}]
'Go early to your tilth if ye would cut it!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:23}]
So they set off, saying privily to each other,

\noindent[\textbf{68:24}]
'There shall surely enter it today unto you no poor person!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:25}]
And they went early deciding to be stingy.

\noindent[\textbf{68:26}]
And when they saw it they said, 'Verily, we have erred!

\noindent[\textbf{68:27}]
Nay, we are forbidden (its fruit)!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:28}]
Said the most moderate of them, 'Said I not to you, "unless ye celebrate God's praises!"'

\noindent[\textbf{68:29}]
Said they, 'Celebrated be the praises of our Lord! verily, we were unjust!'

\noindent[\textbf{68:30}]
And they approached each other with mutual blame.

\noindent[\textbf{68:31}]
Said they, 'O woe to us! verily, we have been outrageous!

\noindent[\textbf{68:32}]
Haply our Lord may give us instead a better than it; verily, we unto our Lord do yearn.'

\section{Surah 18: The Chapter of the Cave (Mecca).}

 (selections)
IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{18:1}]
Praise belongs to God, who sent down to His servant the Book and put no crookedness
therein,---

\noindent[\textbf{18:2}]
straight, to give warning of keen violence from Him; and to give the glad tidings to the
believers, who do what is right, that for them is a goodly reward

\noindent[\textbf{18:3}]
wherein they shall abide for ever and for aye;

\noindent[\textbf{18:4}]
and to give warning to those who say, 'God hath taken to Himself a son.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:5}]
They have no knowledge thereof, nor their fathers; a serious word it is that comes forth
from their mouths! verily, they only speak a lie!

\noindent[\textbf{18:6}]
Haply thou wilt grieve thyself to death for sorrow after them, if they believe not in this
new revelation.

\noindent[\textbf{18:7}]
Verily, we have made what is on the earth an ornament thereof, to try them, which of
them is best in works;

\noindent[\textbf{18:8}]
but, verily, we are going to make what is thereon bare soil.

\noindent[\textbf{18:9}]
Hast thou reckoned that the Fellows of the Cave and Er-raqim were a wonder amongst
our signs?'

\noindent[\textbf{18:10}]
When the youths resorted to the cave and said, 'O our Lord! bring us mercy from Thee,
and dispose for us our affair aright!'

\noindent[\textbf{18:11}]
And we struck their ears (with deafness) in the cave for a number of years.

\noindent[\textbf{18:12}]
Then we raised them up again, that we might know which of the two crews could best
calculate the time of their tarrying.

\noindent[\textbf{18:13}]
We will narrate to thee their story in truth. Verily, they were youths who believed in their
Lord, and we added to their guidance,

\noindent[\textbf{18:14}]
and we braced up their hearts, when they stood up and said, 'Our Lord is the Lord of the
heavens and the earth, we will not call upon any god beside Him, for then we should have said
an extravagant thing.

\noindent[\textbf{18:15}]
These people of ours have taken to other gods beside Him. Though they do not bring any
manifest authority for them. And who is more unjust than he who forges against God a lie?

\noindent[\textbf{18:16}]
'So when ye have gone apart from them and what they serve other than God, then resort
ye to the cave. Our Lord will unfold His mercy to you, and will dispose for you your affair
advantageously.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:17}]
And thou mightst have seen the sun when it rose decline from their cave towards the right
hand, and when it set leave them on the left hand, while they were in the spacious part thereof.
That is one of the signs of God. Whom God guides he is guided indeed, and whom He leads
astray thou shalt surely find for him no patron to guide aright.

\noindent[\textbf{18:18}]
Thou mightst have reckoned them waking though they were sleeping, as we turned them
towards the right and towards the left; and their dog spreading out his fore-paws on the
threshold. Hadst thou come suddenly upon them thou wouldst surely have turned and fled away
from them, and wouldst surely have been filled by them with dread.

\noindent[\textbf{18:19}]
Thus did we raise them up that they might question each other. Spake a speaker amongst
them, 'How long have ye tarried?' They said, 'We have tarried a day or part of a day.' They said,
'Your Lord knows best your tarrying; so send one of you with this coin of yours to the city, and
let him look which of them has purest food, and let him bring you provision thereof; and let him
be subtle and not let any one perceive you.

\noindent[\textbf{18:20}]
Verily, they --- should they perceive you --- would stone you, or would force you back
again unto their faith, and ye would never prosper then.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:21}]
Thus did we make their people acquainted with their story, that they might know that
God's promise is true; and that the Hour, there is no doubt concerning it. When they disputed
amongst themselves concerning their affair, and said, 'Build a building over them, their Lord
knows best about them;' and those who prevailed in their affair said, 'We will surely make a
mosque over them.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:22}]
They will say, 'Three, and the fourth of them was their dog:' and they will say, 'Five, and
the sixth of them was their dog:' guessing at the unseen: and they will say, 'Seven, and the eighth
of them was their dog.' Say, 'My Lord knows best the number of them; none knows them but
few.' Dispute not therefore concerning them save with a plain disputation, and ask not any one of
them concerning them.

\noindent[\textbf{18:23}]
And never say of anything, 'Verily, I am going to do that to-morrow,'

\noindent[\textbf{18:24}]
except 'if God please;' and remember thy Lord when thou hast forgotten, and say,' It
may be that my Lord will guide me to what is nearer to the right than this.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:25}]
They tarried in their cave three hundred years and nine more.

\noindent[\textbf{18:26}]
Say, 'God knows best of their tarrying. His are the unseen things of the heavens and the
earth --- He can see! and hear!' They have no patron beside Him, nor does He let any one share
in His judgment.

\begin{center}
  {\Huge
    *
    \hspace{2em}
    *
    \hspace{2em}
    *
    \hspace{2em}
    *
  }
\end{center}
%TODO use arabic unicode symbols instead?

\noindent[\textbf{18:83}]
And they will ask thee about Dhu'l Qarnain, say, 'I will recite to you a mention of him;

\noindent[\textbf{18:84}]
verily, we established for him in the earth, and we gave him a way to everything;

\noindent[\textbf{18:85}]
and he followed a way

\noindent[\textbf{18:86}]
until when he reached the setting of the sun, he found it setting in a black muddy spring,
and he found thereat a people.' We said, 'O Dhu'l Qarnain! thou mayest either torment these
people, or treat them well.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:87}]
Said he, 'As for him who does wrong, I will torment him, then shall be be sent back to his
Lord, and He will torment him with An unheard---of torment;

\noindent[\textbf{18:88}]
but as for him who believes and acts aright, for him is an excellent reward, and we will
tell him our easy bidding.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:89}]
Then he followed a way

\noindent[\textbf{18:90}]
until when he reached the rising of the sun, he found it rise upon a people to whom we
had given no shelter therefrom.

\noindent[\textbf{18:91}]
So! And we comprehended the knowledge of what (forces) he had with him.

\noindent[\textbf{18:92}]
Then he followed a way

\noindent[\textbf{18:93}]
until when he reached the point between the two mountains, he found below them both a
people who could scarcely understand speech.

\noindent[\textbf{18:94}]
They said, 'O Dhu'l Qarnain! verily, Yagug and Magug are doing evil in the land. Shall
we then pay thee tribute, on condition that thou set between us and them a rampart?'

\noindent[\textbf{18:95}]
He said, 'What my Lord hath established me in is better; so help me with strength, and I
will set between you and them a barrier.

\noindent[\textbf{18:96}]
'Bring me pigs of iron until they fill up the space between the two mountain sides.' Said
he, 'Blow until it makes it a fire.' Said he, 'Bring me, that I may pour over it, molten brass.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:97}]
So they could not scale it, and they could not tunnel it.

\noindent[\textbf{18:98}]
Said he, 'This is a mercy from my Lord; but when the promise of my Lord comes to pass,
He will make it as dust, for the promise of my Lord is true.'

\noindent[\textbf{18:99}]
And we left some of them to surge on that day over others, and the trumpet will be
blown, and we will gather them together.

\section{Surah 17: The Chapter of the Night Journey (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{17:78}]
Be thou steadfast in prayer from the declining of the sun until the dusk of the night, and
the reading of the dawn; verily, the reading of the dawn is ever testified to.

\noindent[\textbf{17:79}]
And for the night, watch thou therein as an extra service. It may be that thy Lord will
raise thee to a laudable station.

\noindent[\textbf{17:80}]
And say, 'O my Lord! make me enter with a just entry; and make me come forth with a
just coming forth; and grant me from, Thee authority to aid.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:81}]
And say, 'Truth has come, and falsehood has vanished! verily, falsehood is transient.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:82}]
And we will send down of the Qur'an that which is a healing and a mercy to the
believers, but it will only increase the wrong-doers in loss.

\noindent[\textbf{17:83}]
And when we favour man he turns away and retires aside, but when evil touches him he
is ever in despair.

\noindent[\textbf{17:84}]
Say, 'Every one acts after his own manner, but your Lord knows, best who is most
guided in the way.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:85}]
They will ask thee of the spirit. Say, 'The spirit comes at the bidding of my Lord, and ye
are given but a little knowledge thereof.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:86}]
If we had wished we would have taken away that with which we have inspired thee; then
thou wouldst have found no guardian against us,

\noindent[\textbf{17:87}]
unless by a mercy from thy Lord; verily, His grace towards thee is great!

\noindent[\textbf{17:88}]
Say, 'If mankind and ginns united together to bring the like of this Qur'an, they could not
bring the like, though they should back each other up!'

\noindent[\textbf{17:89}]
We have turned about for men in this Qur'an every parable; but most men refuse to
accept it, save ungratefully.

\noindent[\textbf{17:90}]
And they say, 'We will by no means believe in thee, until there gush forth for thee a
fountain from the earth;

\noindent[\textbf{17:91}]
or there be made for thee a garden of palms and grapes, and rivers come gushing out
amidst them;

\noindent[\textbf{17:92}]
or thou make the sky to fall down upon us in pieces; or thou bring us God and the angels
before us;

\noindent[\textbf{17:93}]
or there be made for thee a house of gold; or thou climb up into the heaven; and even
then we will not believe in thy climbing there, until thou send down on us a book that we may
read!' Say, 'Celebrated be the praises of my Lord! was I aught but a mortal apostle?'

\noindent[\textbf{17:94}]
Naught prohibited men from believing when the guidance came to them, save their
saying, 'God has sent a mortal for an apostle.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:95}]
Say, 'Were there angels on the earth walking in quiet, we had surely sent them an angel
as an apostle.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:96}]
Say, 'God is witness enough between me and you; verily, He is ever of His servants well
aware, and sees.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:97}]
He whom God guides, he is guided indeed; and he whom God leads astray, thou shalt
never find patrons for them beside Him; and we will gather them upon the resurrection day upon
their faces, blind, and dumb, and deaf; their resort is hell; whenever it grows dull we will give
them another blaze!

\noindent[\textbf{17:98}]
That is their reward for that they disbelieved in our signs, and said, 'What! when we are
bones and rubbish, shall we then be raised up a new creation?'

\noindent[\textbf{17:99}]
Could they not see that God who created the heavens and the earth is able to create the
like of them, and to set for them an appointed time; there is no doubt therein, yet the wrong-doers
refuse to accept it, save ungratefully!

\noindent[\textbf{17:100}]
Say, 'Did ye control the treasuries of the mercy of my Lord, then ye would hold them
through fear of expending; for man is ever niggardly!'

\section{Surah 25: The Chapter of the Discrimination (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{25:1}]
Blessed be He who sent down the Discrimination to His servant that he might be unto the
world a warner;

\noindent[\textbf{25:2}]
whose is the kingdom of the heavens and the earth, and who has not taken to Himself a
son, and who has no partner in His kingdom, and created everything, and then decreed it
determinately!

\noindent[\textbf{25:3}]
And they take beside Him gods who create not aught, but are themselves created, and
cannot control for themselves harm or profit, and cannot control death, or life, or resurrection.

\noindent[\textbf{25:4}]
And those who misbelieve say, 'This is nothing but a lie which he has forged, and
another people hath helped him at it;' but they have wrought an injustice and a falsehood.

\noindent[\textbf{25:5}]
And they say, 'Old folks' tales, which he has got written down while they are dictated to
him morning and evening.'

\noindent[\textbf{25:6}]
Say, 'He sent it down who knows the secret in the heavens and the earth; verily, He is
ever forgiving, merciful!'

\noindent[\textbf{25:7}]
And they say, 'What ails this prophet that he eats food and walks in the markets?---
unless there be sent down to him an angel and be a warner with him ....

\noindent[\textbf{25:8}]
Or there be thrown to him a treasury or he have a garden to eat therefrom .... !' and the
unjust say, 'Ye only follow an infatuated man.'

\noindent[\textbf{25:9}]
See how they strike out for thee parables, and err, and cannot find a way.

\noindent[\textbf{25:10}]
Blessed be He who, if He please, can make for thee better than that, gardens beneath
which rivers flow, and can make for thee castles!

\noindent[\textbf{25:11}]
Nay, but they call the Hour a lie; but we have prepared for those who call the Hour a lie a
blaze:

\noindent[\textbf{25:12}]
when it seizes them from a far-off place they shall hear its raging and roaring;

\noindent[\textbf{25:13}]
and when they are thrown into a narrow place thereof, fastened together, they shall call
there for destruction.

\noindent[\textbf{25:14}]
Call not today for one destruction, but call, for many destructions!

\noindent[\textbf{25:15}]
Say, 'Is that better or the garden of eternity which was promised to those who fear ---
which is ever for them a recompense and a retreat?'

\noindent[\textbf{25:16}]
They shall have therein what they please, to dwell therein for aye: that is of thy Lord a
promise to be demanded.

\noindent[\textbf{25:17}]
And the day He shall gather them and what they served beside God, and He shall say,
'Was it ye who led my servants here astray, or did they err from the way?'

\noindent[\textbf{25:18}]
They shall say, 'Celebrated be Thy praise, it was not befitting for us to take any patrons
but Thee; but Thou didst give them and their fathers enjoyment until they forgot the Reminder
and were a lost people!'

\noindent[\textbf{25:19}]
And now have they proved you liars for what ye say, and they cannot ward off or help.
And he of you who does wrong we will make him taste great torment.

\noindent[\textbf{25:20}]
We have not sent before thee any messengers but that they ate food and walked in the
markets; but we have made some of you a trial to others: will ye be patient? thy Lord doth ever
look.

\noindent[\textbf{25:21}]
And those who do not hope to meet us say, 'Unless the angels be sent down to us, or we
see our Lord....!' They are too big with pride in their souls and they have exceeded with a great
excess!

\noindent[\textbf{25:22}]
The day they shall see the angels,--- no glad tidings on that day for the sinners, and they
shall say, 'It is rigorously forbidden!'

\noindent[\textbf{25:23}]
And we will go on to the works which they have done, and make them like motes in a
sunbeam scattered!

\noindent[\textbf{25:24}]
The fellows of Paradise on that day shall be in a better abiding-place and a better
noonday rest.

\noindent[\textbf{25:25}]
The day the heavens shall be cleft asunder with the clouds, and the angels shall be sent
down descending.

\noindent[\textbf{25:26}]
The true kingdom on that day shall belong to the Merciful, and it shall be a hard day for
the misbelievers.

\noindent[\textbf{25:27}]
And the day when the unjust shall bite his hands and say, 'O, would that I had taken a
way with the Apostle!'

\noindent[\textbf{25:28}]
O, woe is me! would that I had not taken such a one for a friend now,

\noindent[\textbf{25:29}]
for he did lead me astray from the Reminder after it had come to me, for Satan leaves
man in the lurch!'

\section{Surah 13: The Chapter of Thunder (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{13:30}]
Thus have we sent thee to a nation before which other nations have passed away, to recite
to them that which we have inspired thee with; yet they misbelieve in the merciful! Say, 'He is
my Lord; there is no god but He; upon Him do I rely, and unto Him is my repentance.'

\noindent[\textbf{13:31}]
And though it were a Qur'an by which the mountains were moved, or by which the earth
were cut up, or the dead made to speak --- nay, God's is the command altogether! Did not those
who believed know that if God had pleased He would have guided men altogether? And a
striking calamity shall not cease to overtake those who misbelieve for what they have wrought,
or to alight close by their dwellings; until God's promise comes --- verily, God fails not in His
promise.

\noindent[\textbf{13:32}]
Before thee have apostles been mocked at; and those who misbelieved have I allowed to
range at large; and then it caught them up! How then was my punishment?

\noindent[\textbf{13:33}]
Shall He who is standing over every soul (to note) what it has earned ---? And they join
partners with God! Say, 'Name them; can ye inform Him of what He does not know in the earth?
or is it for name's sake only (that ye call upon them)? 'Nay, then, stratagem is made seemly to
those who misbelieve, and they turn folks from the path of God! But whomsoever God doth lead
astray, no guide has he.'

\section{Surah 34: The Chapter of Seba (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{34:43}]
And when our signs are recited to them they say, 'This is only a man who wishes to turn
you from what your fathers served;' and they say, 'This is only a lie forged,' and those who
misbelieve will say of the truth when it comes to them, 'It is only obvious sorcery!'

\noindent[\textbf{34:44}]
But we have not brought them any book which they may study, and we have not sent to
them before thee a warner.

\noindent[\textbf{34:45}]
Those before them said it was a lie, and these have not reached a tithe of what we had
given them. And they said my apostles were liars, and how great a change was then!

\noindent[\textbf{34:46}]
Say, 'I only admonish you of one thing, that ye should stand up before God in twos or
singly, and then that ye reflect that there is no ginn in your companion. He is only a warner to
you before the keen torment.'

\noindent[\textbf{34:47}]
Say, 'I do not ask you for it a hire; that is for yourselves; my hire is only from God, and
He is witness over all.'

\noindent[\textbf{34:48}]
Say, 'Verily, my Lord hurls forth the truth; and He well knows the unseen.'

\noindent[\textbf{34:49}]
Say, 'The truth has come, and falsehood shall vanish and shall not come back.'

\noindent[\textbf{34:50}]
Say, 'If I err I only err against myself; and if I am guided it is all what my Lord inspires
me; verily, He is the hearing, the nigh!'

\section{Surah 17: The Chapter of the Night Journey (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{17:105}]
'In truth have we sent it down, and in truth has it come down; and we have not sent thee
as aught but a herald of glad tidings and a warner.

\noindent[\textbf{17:106}]
'And a Qur'an which we have divided, that thou mayst read it to mankind leisurely, and
we sent it down, sending it down.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:107}]
Say, 'Believe ye therein, or believe not; verily, those who were given the knowledge
before it, when it is read to them fall down upon their beards adoring!

\noindent[\textbf{17:108}]
and they say, "Celebrated be the praises of our Lord! verily, the promise of our Lord is
ever fulfilled"---

\noindent[\textbf{17:109}]
they fall down upon their beards weeping, and it increases their humility.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:110}]
Say, 'Call on God, or call on the Merciful One, whichever ye may call on Him by; for
His are the best of names.' And do not say thy prayers openly, nor yet murmur them, but seek a
way between these.

\noindent[\textbf{17:111}]
And say, 'Praise belongs to God, who has not taken to Himself a son, and has not had a
partner in His kingdom, nor had a patron against (such) abasement.' And magnify Him greatly!

\clearpage
\chapter{Session 5}


\section{Surah 55: The Chapter of the Merciful (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{55:1}]
The Merciful

\noindent[\textbf{55:2}]
taught the Qur'an;

\noindent[\textbf{55:3}]
He created man,

\noindent[\textbf{55:4}]
taught him plain speech.

\noindent[\textbf{55:5}]
The sun and the moon have their appointed time;

\noindent[\textbf{55:6}]
The herbs and the trees adore;

\noindent[\textbf{55:7}]
And the heavens, He raised them and set the balance,

\noindent[\textbf{55:8}]
that ye should not be outrageous in the balance;

\noindent[\textbf{55:9}]
But weigh ye aright, and stint not the balance.

\noindent[\textbf{55:10}]
And the earth He has set it for living creatures;

\noindent[\textbf{55:11}]
therein are fruits and palms, with sheaths;

\noindent[\textbf{55:12}]
and grain with chaff and frequent shoots;

\noindent[\textbf{55:13}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:14}]
He created men of crackling clay like the potters.

\noindent[\textbf{55:15}]
And He created the ginn from smokeless fire.

\noindent[\textbf{55:16}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:17}]
The Lord of the two easts and the Lord of the two wests!

\noindent[\textbf{55:18}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:19}]
He has let loose the two seas that meet together;

\noindent[\textbf{55:20}]
between them is a barrier they cannot pass!

\noindent[\textbf{55:21}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:22}]
He brings forth from each pearls both large and small!

\noindent[\textbf{55:23}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:24}]
His are the ships which rear aloft in the sea like mountains.

\noindent[\textbf{55:25}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:26}]
Every one upon it is transient,

\noindent[\textbf{55:27}]
but the face of thy Lord endowed with majesty and honour shall endure.

\noindent[\textbf{55:28}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\noindent[\textbf{55:29}]
Of Him whosoever is in the heaven and the earth does beg; every day He is in (some
fresh) business!

\noindent[\textbf{55:30}]
Then which of your Lord's bounties will ye twain deny?

\section{Surah 17: The Chapter of the Night Journey (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{17:45}]
And when thou readest the Qur'an we place between thee and those who believe not in
the hereafter a covering veil.

\noindent[\textbf{17:46}]
And we place covers upon their hearts, lest they should understand, and dulness in their
ears. And when thou dost mention in the Qur'an thy Lord by Himself they turn their backs in
aversion.

\noindent[\textbf{17:47}]
We know best for what they listen when they listen to thee; and when they whisper apart
--- when the wrongdoers say, 'Ye only follow a man enchanted.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:48}]
Behold, how they strike out for you parables, and err, and cannot find the way!

\noindent[\textbf{17:49}]
They say, 'What! when we have become bones and rubbish are we to be raised up a new
creature?'

\noindent[\textbf{17:50}]
Say, 'Be ye stones, or iron,

\noindent[\textbf{17:51}]
or a creature, the greatest your breasts can conceive ---!' Then they shall say, 'Who is to
restore us?' Say, 'He who originated you at first;' and they will wag their heads and say, 'When
will that be?' Say, 'It may, perhaps, be nigh.'

\noindent[\textbf{17:52}]
The day when He shall call on you and ye shall answer with praise to Him, and they will
think that they have tarried but a little.

\noindent[\textbf{17:53}]
And say to my servants that they speak in a kind way; verily, Satan makes ill-will
between them; verily, Satan was ever unto man an open foe.

\noindent[\textbf{17:54}]
Your Lord knows you best; if He please He will have mercy upon you, or if He please He
will torment you: but we, have not sent thee to take charge of them.

\section{Surah 19: The Chapter of Mary (Mecca).}

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{19:1}]
KAF HA YA AIN SAD.

\noindent[\textbf{19:2}]
The mention of thy Lord's mercy to His servant Zachariah,

\noindent[\textbf{19:3}]
when he called on his Lord with a secret calling.

\noindent[\textbf{19:4}]
Said he, 'My Lord! verily, my bones are weak, and my head flares with hoariness; --- and
I never was unfortunate in my prayers to Thee, my Lord!

\noindent[\textbf{19:5}]
But I fear my heirs after me, and my wife is barren; then grant me from Thee a successor,

\noindent[\textbf{19:6}]
to be my heir and the heir of the family of Jacob, and make him, my Lord! acceptable.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:7}]
'O Zachariah! verily, we, give thee glad tidings of a son, whose name shall be John. We
never made a namesake of his before.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:8}]
Said he, 'My Lord! how can I have a son, when my wife is barren, and I have reached
through old age to decrepitude?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:9}]
He said, 'Thus says thy Lord, It is easy for Me, for I created thee at first when yet thou
wast nothing.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:10}]
Said he, 'O my Lord! make for me a sign.' He said, 'Thy sign is that thou shalt not speak
to men for three nights (though) sound.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:11}]
Then he went forth unto his people from the chamber, and he made signs to them:
'Celebrate (God's) praises morning and evening!'

\noindent[\textbf{19:12}]
'O John! take the Book with strength;' and we gave him judgment when a boy,

\noindent[\textbf{19:13}]
and grace from us, and purity; and he was pious

\noindent[\textbf{19:14}]
and righteous to his parents, and was not a rebellious tyrant.

\noindent[\textbf{19:15}]
So peace upon him the day he was born, and the day he died, and the day he shall be
raised up alive.

\noindent[\textbf{19:16}]
And mention, in the Book, Mary; when she retired from her family into an eastern place;

\noindent[\textbf{19:17}]
and she took a veil (to screen herself) from them; and we sent unto her our spirit; and he
took for her the semblance of a well-made man.

\noindent[\textbf{19:18}]
Said she, 'Verily, I take refuge in the Merciful One from thee, if thou art pious.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:19}]
Said he, 'I am only a messenger of thy Lord to bestow on thee a pure boy.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:20}]
Said she, 'How can I have a boy when no man has touched me, and when I am no
harlot?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:21}]
He said, 'Thus says thy Lord, It is easy for Me! and we will make him a sign unto man,
and a mercy from us; for it is a decided matter.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:22}]
So she conceived him, and she retired with him into a remote place.

\noindent[\textbf{19:23}]
And the labour pains came upon her at the trunk of a palm tree, and she said, 'O that I
had died before this, and been forgotten out of mind!'

\noindent[\textbf{19:24}]
and he called to her from beneath her, 'Grieve not, for thy Lord has placed a stream
beneath thy feet;

\noindent[\textbf{19:25}]
and shake towards thee the trunk of the palm tree, it will drop upon thee fresh dates fit to
gather;

\noindent[\textbf{19:26}]
so eat, and drink, and cheer thine eye; and if thou shouldst see any mortal say, "Verily, I
have vowed to the Merciful One a fast, and I will not speak today with a human being."'

\noindent[\textbf{19:27}]
Then she brought it to her people, carrying it; said they, 'O Mary! thou hast done an
extraordinary thing!

\noindent[\textbf{19:28}]
O sister of Aaron! thy father was not a bad man, nor was thy mother a harlot!'

\noindent[\textbf{19:29}]
And she pointed to him, and they said, 'How are we to speak with one who is in the
cradle a child?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:30}]
He said, 'Verily, I am a servant of God; He has brought me the Book, and He has made
me a prophet,

\noindent[\textbf{19:31}]
and He has made me blessed wherever I be; and He has required of me prayer and
almsgiving so long as I live,

\noindent[\textbf{19:32}]
and piety towards my mother, and has not made me a miserable tyrant;

\noindent[\textbf{19:33}]
and peace upon me the day I was born, and the day I die, and the day I shall be raised up
alive.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:34}]
That is, Jesus the son of Mary, --- by the word of truth whereon ye do dispute!

\noindent[\textbf{19:35}]
God could not take to himself any son! celebrated be His praise! when He decrees a
matter He only says to it, 'BE,' and it is;

\noindent[\textbf{19:36}]
and, verily, God is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him; this is the right way.

\noindent[\textbf{19:37}]
And the parties have disagreed amongst themselves, but woe to those who disbelieve,
from the witnessing of the mighty day!

\noindent[\textbf{19:38}]
they can hear and they can see, on the day when they shall come to us; but the evildoers
are today in obvious error!

\noindent[\textbf{19:39}]
And warn them of the day of sighing, when the matter is decreed while they are heedless,
and while they do not believe.

\noindent[\textbf{19:40}]
Verily, we will inherit the earth and all who are upon it, and unto us shall they return!

\noindent[\textbf{19:41}]
And mention, in the Book, Abraham; verily, he was a confessors,--- a prophet.

\noindent[\textbf{19:42}]
When he said to his father, 'O my sire! why dost thou worship what can neither hear nor
see nor avail thee aught?

\noindent[\textbf{19:43}]
O my sire! verily, to me has come knowledge which has not come to thee; then follow
me, and I will guide thee to a level way.

\noindent[\textbf{19:44}]
'O my sire! serve not Satan; verily, Satan is ever a rebel against the Merciful.

\noindent[\textbf{19:45}]
O my sire! verily,. I fear that there may touch thee torment from the Merciful, and that
thou mayest be a client of Satan.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:46}]
Said he, 'What! art thou averse from my gods, O Abraham? verily, if thou dost not desist
I will certainly stone thee; but get thee gone from me for a time!'

\noindent[\textbf{19:47}]
Said he, 'Peace be upon thee! I will ask forgiveness for thee from my Lord; verily, He is
very gracious to me:

\noindent[\textbf{19:48}]
but I will part from you and what ye call on beside God, and will pray my Lord that I be
not unfortunate in my prayer to my Lord.'

\noindent[\textbf{19:49}]
And when he had parted from them and what they served beside God, we granted him
Isaac and Jacob, and each of them we made a prophet;

\noindent[\textbf{19:50}]
and we granted them of our mercy, and we made the tongue of truth lofty for them.

\noindent[\textbf{19:51}]
And mention, in the Book, Moses; verily, he was sincere, and was an apostle,--- a
prophet.

\noindent[\textbf{19:52}]
We called him from the right side of the mountain; and we made him draw nigh unto us
to commune with him,

\noindent[\textbf{19:53}]
and we granted him, of our mercy, his brother Aaron as a prophet.

\noindent[\textbf{19:54}]
And mention, in the Book, Ishmael; verily, he was true to his promise, and was an
apostle,--- a prophet;

\noindent[\textbf{19:55}]
and he used to bid his people prayers and almsgiving, and was acceptable in the sight of
his Lord.

\noindent[\textbf{19:56}]
And mention, in the Book, Idris; verily, he was a confessors prophet,--- a prophet;

\noindent[\textbf{19:57}]
and we raised him to a lofty place.

\noindent[\textbf{19:58}]
These are those to whom God has been gracious, of the prophets of the seed of Adam,
and of those whom we bore with Noah, and of the seed of Abraham and Israel, and of those we
guided and elected; when the signs of the Merciful are read to them, they fall down adoring and
weeping.

\noindent[\textbf{19:59}]
And successors succeeded them, who lost sight of prayer and followed lusts, but they
shall at length find themselves going wrong,

\noindent[\textbf{19:60}]
except such as repent and believe and act aright; for these shall enter Paradise, and shall
not be wronged at all,---

\noindent[\textbf{19:61}]
gardens of Eden, which the Merciful has promised to His servants in the unseen; verily,
His promise ever comes to pass!

\noindent[\textbf{19:62}]
They shall hear no empty talk therein, but only 'peace;' and they shall have their
provision therein, morning and evening;

\noindent[\textbf{19:63}]
that is Paradise which we will give for an inheritance to those of our servants who are
pious!

\noindent[\textbf{19:64}]
We do not descend save at the bidding of thy Lord; His is what is before us, and what is
behind us, and what is between those; for thy Lord is never forgetful,---

\noindent[\textbf{19:65}]
the Lord of the heavens and the earth, and of what is between the two; then serve Him
and persevere in His service. Dost thou know a namesake of His?

\noindent[\textbf{19:66}]
Man will say, 'What! when I have died shall I then come forth alive?

\noindent[\textbf{19:67}]
Does not man then remember that we created him before when he was naught?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:68}]
And by thy Lord! we will surely gather them together, and the devils too; then we will
surely bring them forward around hell, on their knees!

\noindent[\textbf{19:69}]
Then we will drag off from every sect whichever of them has been most bold against the
Merciful.

\noindent[\textbf{19:70}]
Then we know best which of them deserves most to be broiled therein.

\noindent[\textbf{19:71}]
There is not one of you who will not go down to it,--- that is settled and decided by thy
Lord.

\noindent[\textbf{19:72}]
Then we will save those who fear us; but we will leave the evildoers therein on their
knees.

\noindent[\textbf{19:73}]
And when our signs are recited to them manifest, those who misbelieve say to those who
believe, 'Which of the two parties is best placed and in the best company?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:74}]
And how many generations before them have we destroyed who were better off in
property and appearance?

\noindent[\textbf{19:75}]
Say, 'Whosoever is in error, let the Merciful extend to him length of days!--- until they
see what they are threatened with, whether it be the torment or whether it be the Hour, then they
shall know who is worse placed and weakest in forces!'

\noindent[\textbf{19:76}]
And those who are guided God will increase in guidance. And enduring good works are
best with thy Lord for a reward, and best for restoration.

\noindent[\textbf{19:77}]
Hast thou seen him who disbelieves in our signs, and says, 'I shall surely be given wealth
and children?'

\noindent[\textbf{19:78}]
Has he become acquainted with the unseen, or has he taken a compact with the Merciful?

\noindent[\textbf{19:79}]
Not so! We will write down what he says, and we will extend to him a length of torment,

\noindent[\textbf{19:80}]
and we will make him inherit what he says, and he shall come to us alone.

\noindent[\textbf{19:81}]
They take other gods besides God to be their glory.

\noindent[\textbf{19:82}]
Not so! They shall deny their worship and shall be opponents of theirs!

\noindent[\textbf{19:83}]
Dost thou not see that we have sent the devils against the misbelievers, to drive them on
to sin?

\noindent[\textbf{19:84}]
but, be not thou hasty with them. Verily, we will number them a number (of days),---

\noindent[\textbf{19:85}]
the day when we will gather the pious to the Merciful as ambassadors,

\noindent[\textbf{19:86}]
and we will drive the sinners to hell like (herds) to water!

\noindent[\textbf{19:87}]
They shall not possess intercession, save he who has taken a compact with the Merciful.

\noindent[\textbf{19:88}]
They say, 'The Merciful has taken to Himself a son:' ---

\noindent[\textbf{19:89}]
ye have brought a monstrous thing!

\noindent[\textbf{19:90}]
The heavens well-nigh burst asunder thereat, and the earth is riven, and the mountains fall
down broken,

\noindent[\textbf{19:91}]
that they attribute to the Merciful a son!

\noindent[\textbf{19:92}]
but it, becomes not the Merciful to take to Himself a son!

\noindent[\textbf{19:93}]
there is none in the heavens or the earth but comes to the Merciful as a servant;

\noindent[\textbf{19:94}]
He counts them and numbers them by number,

\noindent[\textbf{19:95}]
and they are all coming to Him on the resurrection day singly.

\noindent[\textbf{19:96}]
Verily, those who believe and act aright, to them the Merciful will give love.

\noindent[\textbf{19:97}]
We have only made it easy for thy tongue that thou mayest thereby give glad tidings to
the pious, and warn thereby a contentious people.

\noindent[\textbf{19:98}]
How many a Generation before them have we destroyed? Canst thou find any one of
them, or hear a whisper of them?

\section{Surah 20: The Chapter of TA HA (Mecca).}

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{20:1}]
TA HA.

\noindent[\textbf{20:2}]
We have not sent down this Qur'an to thee that thou shouldst be wretched;

\noindent[\textbf{20:3}]
only as a reminder to him who fears ---

\noindent[\textbf{20:4}]
descending from Him who created the earth and the high heavens,

\noindent[\textbf{20:5}]
the Merciful settled on the throne!

\noindent[\textbf{20:6}]
His are what is in the heavens, and what is in the earth, and what is between the two, and
what is beneath the ground!

\noindent[\textbf{20:7}]
And if thou art public in thy speech --- yet, verily, he knows the secret, and more hidden
still.

\noindent[\textbf{20:8}]
God, there is no god but He! His are the excellent names.

\noindent[\textbf{20:9}]
Has the story of Moses come to thee?

\noindent[\textbf{20:10}]
When he saw the fire and said to his family, 'Tarry ye; verily, I perceive a fire! Haply I
may bring you therefrom a brand, or may find guidance by the fire.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:11}]
And when he came to it he was called to, 'O Moses!

\noindent[\textbf{20:12}]
verily, I am thy Lord, so take off thy sandals; verily, thou art in the holy valley Tuva,

\noindent[\textbf{20:13}]
and I have chosen thee. So listen to what is inspired thee;

\noindent[\textbf{20:14}]
verily, I am God, there is no god but Me! then serve Me, and be steadfast in prayer to
remember Me.

\noindent[\textbf{20:15}]
'Verily, the hour is coming, I almost make it appear, that every soul may be recompensed
for its efforts.

\noindent[\textbf{20:16}]
'Let not then him who believes not therein and follows his lusts ever turn thee away
therefrom, and thou be ruined.

\noindent[\textbf{20:17}]
'What is that in thy right hand, O Moses?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:18}]
Said he, 'It is my staff on which I lean, and wherewith I beat down leaves for my flocks,
and for which I have other uses.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:19}]
Said He, 'Throw it down, O Moses!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:20}]
and he threw it down, and behold! it was a snake that moved about.

\noindent[\textbf{20:21}]
Said He, 'Take hold of it and fear not; we will restore it to its first state.

\noindent[\textbf{20:22}]
'But press thy hand to thy side, it shall come forth white without harm, --- another sign!

\noindent[\textbf{20:23}]
to show thee of our great signs!

\noindent[\textbf{20:24}]
'Go unto Pharaoh, verily, he is outrageous!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:25}]
Said he, 'My Lord! expand for me my breast;

\noindent[\textbf{20:26}]
and make what I am bidden easy to me;

\noindent[\textbf{20:27}]
and loose the knot from my tongue,

\noindent[\textbf{20:28}]
that they may understand my speech;

\noindent[\textbf{20:29}]
and make for me a minister from my people,---

\noindent[\textbf{20:30}]
Aaron my brother;

\noindent[\textbf{20:31}]
gird up my loins through him,

\noindent[\textbf{20:32}]
and join him with me in the affair;

\noindent[\textbf{20:33}]
that we may celebrate Thy praises much

\noindent[\textbf{20:34}]
and remember Thee much.

\noindent[\textbf{20:35}]
'Verily, Thou dost ever behold us!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:36}]
He said, 'Thou art granted thy request, O Moses!

\noindent[\textbf{20:37}]
and we have already shown favours unto thee at another time.

\noindent[\textbf{20:38}]
When we inspired thy mother with what we inspired her,

\noindent[\textbf{20:39}]
"Hurl him into the ark, and hurl him into the sea; and the sea shall cast him on the shore,
and an enemy of mine and of his shall take him;" --- for on thee have I cast my love, that thou
mayest be formed under my eye.

\noindent[\textbf{20:40}]
When thy sister walked on and said, "Shall I guide you to one who will take charge of
him?" And we restored thee to thy mother, that her eye might be cheered and that she should not
grieve. And thou didst slay a person and we saved thee from the trouble, and we tried thee with
various trials. And thou didst tarry for years amongst the people of Midian; then thou didst come
(hither) at (our) decree, O Moses!

\noindent[\textbf{20:41}]
And I have chosen thee for myself.

\noindent[\textbf{20:42}]
Go, thou and thy brother, with my signs, and be not remiss in remembering me.

\noindent[\textbf{20:43}]
Go ye both to Pharaoh; verily, he is outrageous!

\noindent[\textbf{20:44}]
and speak to him a gentle speech, haply he may be mindful or may fear.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:45}]
They two said, 'Our Lord! verily, we fear that he may trespass against us, or that he may
be outrageous.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:46}]
He said, 'Fear not; verily, I am with you twain. I hear and see!

\noindent[\textbf{20:47}]
'So come ye to him and say, "Verily, we are the apostles of thy Lord; send then the
children of Israel with us; and do not torment them. We have brought thee a sign from thy Lord,
and peace be upon him who follows the guidance!

\noindent[\textbf{20:48}]
'"Verily, we are inspired that the torment will surely come upon him who calls us liars
and turns his back."'

\noindent[\textbf{20:49}]
Said he, 'And who is your Lord, O Moses?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:50}]
He said, 'Our Lord is He who gave everything its creation, then guided it.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:51}]
Said he, 'And what of the former generations?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:52}]
He said 'The knowledge of them is with my Lord in a Book; my Lord misleads not, nor
forgets!

\noindent[\textbf{20:53}]
Who made for you the earth a bed; and has traced for you paths therein; and has sent
down from the sky water, --- and we have brought forth thereby divers sorts of different
vegetables.

\noindent[\textbf{20:54}]
Eat and pasture your cattle therefrom; verily, in that are signs to those endued with
intelligence.

\noindent[\textbf{20:55}]
From it have we created you and into it will we send you back, and from it will we bring
you forth another time.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:56}]
We did show him our signs, all of them, but he called them lies and did refuse.

\noindent[\textbf{20:57}]
Said he, 'Hast thou come to us, to turn us out of our land with thy magic, O Moses?

\noindent[\textbf{20:58}]
Then we will bring you magic like it; and we will make between us and thee an
appointment; we will not break it, nor do thou either;---a fair place.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:59}]
Said he, 'Let your appointment be for the day of adornment, and let the people assemble
in the forenoon.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:60}]
But Pharaoh turned his back, and collected his tricks, and then he came.

\noindent[\textbf{20:61}]
Said Moses to them, 'Woe to you! do not forge against God a lie; lest He destroy you by
torment; for disappointed has ever been he who has forged.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:62}]
And they argued their matter among themselves; and secretly talked it over.

\noindent[\textbf{20:63}]
Said they, 'These twain are certainly two magicians, who wish to turn you out of your
land by their magic, and to remove your most exemplary doctrine.

\noindent[\textbf{20:64}]
Collect therefore your tricks, and then form a row; for he is prosperous today who has the
upper hand.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:65}]
Said they, 'O Moses! either thou must throw, or we must be the first to throw.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:66}]
He said, 'Nay, throw ye!' and lo! their ropes and their staves appeared to move along.

\noindent[\textbf{20:67}]
And Moses felt a secret fear within his soul.

\noindent[\textbf{20:68}]
Said we, 'Fear not! thou shalt have the upper hand.

\noindent[\textbf{20:69}]
Throw down what is in thy right hand; and it shall devour what they have made. Verily,
what they have made is but a magician's trick; and no magician shall prosper wherever he
comes.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:70}]
And the magicians were cast down in adoration; said they, 'We believe in the Lord of
Aaron and of Moses!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:71}]
Said he, 'Do ye believe in Him before I give you leave? Verily, he is your master who
taught you magic! Therefore will I surely cut off your hands and feet on alternate sides, and I
will surely crucify you on the trunks of palm trees; and ye shall surely know which of us is
keenest at torment and more lasting.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:72}]
Said they, 'We will never prefer thee to what has come to us of manifest signs, and to
Him who originated us. Decide then what thou canst decide; thou canst only decide in the life of
this world!

\noindent[\textbf{20:73}]
Verily, we believe in our Lord, that He may pardon us our sins, and the magic thou hast
forced us to use; and God is better and more lasting!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:74}]
Verily, he who comes to his Lord a sinner,--- verily, for him is hell; he shall not die
therein, and shall not live.

\noindent[\textbf{20:75}]
But he who comes to Him a believer who has done aright --- these, for them are the
highest ranks,---

\noindent[\textbf{20:76}]
gardens of Eden beneath which rivers flow, to dwell therein for aye; for that is the reward
of him who keeps pure.

\noindent[\textbf{20:77}]
And we inspired Moses, 'Journey by night with my servants, and strike out for them a dry
road in the sea. Fear not pursuit, nor be afraid!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:78}]
Then Pharaoh followed them with his armies, and there overwhelmed them of the sea that
which overwhelmed them.

\noindent[\textbf{20:79}]
And Pharaoh and his people went astray and were not guided.

\noindent[\textbf{20:80}]
O children of Israel! We have saved you from your enemy; and we made an appointment
with you on the right side of the mount; and we sent down upon you the manna and the quails.

\noindent[\textbf{20:81}]
'Eat of the good things we have provided you with, and do not exceed therein, lest my
wrath light upon you; for whomsoever my wrath lights upon he falls!

\noindent[\textbf{20:82}]
'Yet am I forgiving unto him who repents and believes and does right, and then is guided.

\noindent[\textbf{20:83}]
'But what has hastened thee on away from thy people, O Moses?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:84}]
He said, 'They were here upon my track and I hastened on to Thee, my Lord! that thou
mightest be pleased.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:85}]
Said He, 'Verily, we have tried thy people, since thou didst leave, and es Samariy has led
them astray.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:86}]
And Moses returned to his people, wrathful, grieving! Said he, 'O my, people! did not
your Lord promise you a good promise? Has the time seemed too long for you, or do you desire
that wrath should light on you from your Lord, that ye have broken your promise to me?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:87}]
They said, 'We have not broken our promise to thee of our own accord. But we were
made to carry loads of the ornaments of the people, and we hurled them down, and so did es
Samariy cast;

\noindent[\textbf{20:88}]
and he brought forth for the people a corporeal calf which lowed.' And they said, 'This is
your god and the god of Moses, but he has forgotten!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:89}]
What! do they not see that it does not return them any speech, and cannot control for
them harm or profit?

\noindent[\textbf{20:90}]
Aaron too told them before, 'O my people! ye are only being tried thereby; and, verily,
your Lord is the Merciful, so follow me and obey my bidding.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:91}]
They said, 'We will not cease to pay devotion to it until Moses come back to us.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:92}]
Said he, 'O Aaron! what prevented thee, when thou didst see them go astray,

\noindent[\textbf{20:93}]
from following me? Hast thou then rebelled against my bidding?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:94}]
Said he, 'O son of my mother! seize me not by my beard, or my head! Verily, I feared
lest thou shouldst say, "Thou hast made a division amongst the children of Israel, and hast not
observed my word."'

\noindent[\textbf{20:95}]
Said he, 'What was thy design, O Samariy?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:96}]
Said he, 'I beheld what they beheld not, and I grasped a handful from the footprint of the
messenger and cast it; for thus my soul induced me.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:97}]
Said he, 'Then get thee gone; verily, it shall be thine in life to say, "Touch me not!" and,
verily, for thee there is a threat which thou shalt surely never alter. But look at thy god to which
thou wert just now devout; we will surely burn it, and then we will scatter it in scattered pieces in
the sea.

\noindent[\textbf{20:98}]
'Your God is only God who,--- there is no god but He,--- He embraceth everything in His
knowledge.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:99}]
Thus do we narrate to thee the history of what has gone before, and we have brought thee
a reminder from us.

\noindent[\textbf{20:100}]
Whoso turns therefrom, verily, he shall bear on the resurrection day a burden:---

\noindent[\textbf{20:101}]
for them to bear for aye, and evil for them on the resurrection day will it be to bear.

\noindent[\textbf{20:102}]
On the day when the trumpet shall be blown, and we will gather the sinners in that day
blue-eyed.

\noindent[\textbf{20:103}]
They shall whisper to each other, 'Ye have only tarried ten days.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:104}]
We know best what they say, when the most exemplary of them in his way shall say, 'Ye
have only tarried a day.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:105}]
They will ask thee about the mountains; say, 'My Lord will scatter them in scattered
pieces,

\noindent[\textbf{20:106}]
and He will leave them a level plain,

\noindent[\textbf{20:107}]
thou wilt see therein no crookedness or inequality.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:108}]
On that day they shall follow the caller in whom is no crookedness; and the voices shall
be hushed before the Merciful, and thou shalt hear naught but a shuffling.

\noindent[\textbf{20:109}]
On that day shall no intercession be of any avail, save from such as the Merciful permits,
and who is acceptable to Him in speech.

\noindent[\textbf{20:110}]
He knows what is before them and what is behind them, but they do not comprehend
knowledge of Him.

\noindent[\textbf{20:111}]
Faces shall be humbled before the Living, the Self-subsistent; and he who bears injustice
is ever lost.

\noindent[\textbf{20:112}]
But he who does righteous acts and is a believer, he shall fear neither wrong nor
diminution.

\noindent[\textbf{20:113}]
Thus have we sent it down an Arabic Qur'an; and we have turned about in it the threat,---
haply they may fear, or it may cause them to remember.

\noindent[\textbf{20:114}]
Exalted then be God, the king, the truth! Hasten not the Qur'an before its inspiration is
decided for thee; but say, 'O Lord! increase me in knowledge.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:115}]
We did make a covenant with Adam of yore, but he forgot it, and we found no firm
purpose in him.

\noindent[\textbf{20:116}]
And when we said to the angels, ' Adore Adam,' they adored, save Iblis, who refused.

\noindent[\textbf{20:117}]
And we said, 'O Adam! verily, this is a foe to thee and to thy wife; never then let him
drive you twain forth from the garden or thou wilt be wretched.

\noindent[\textbf{20:118}]
Verily, thou hast not to be hungry there, nor naked!

\noindent[\textbf{20:119}]
and, verily, thou shalt not thirst therein, nor feel the noonday heat!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:120}]
But the devil whispered to him. Said he, 'O Adam! shall I guide thee to the tree of
immortality, and a kingdom that shall not wane?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:121}]
And they eat therefrom, and their shame became apparent to them; and they began to
stitch upon themselves some leaves of the garden; and Adam rebelled against his Lord, and went
astray.

\noindent[\textbf{20:122}]
Then his Lord chose him, and relented towards him, and guided him.

\noindent[\textbf{20:123}]
Said he, 'Go down, ye twain, therefrom altogether, some of you foes to the other. And if
there should come to you, from me a guidance; then whoso follows my guidance shall neither err
nor be wretched.

\noindent[\textbf{20:124}]
But he who turns away from my reminder, verily, for him shall be a straitened livelihood;
and we will gather him on the resurrection day blind!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:125}]
He shall say, 'My Lord! wherefore hast Thou gathered me blind when I used to see?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:126}]
He shall say, 'Our signs came to thee, and thou didst forget them; thus today art thou
forgotten!'

\noindent[\textbf{20:127}]
Thus do we recompense him who is extravagant and believes not in the signs of his Lord;
and the torment of the hereafter is keener and more lasting!

\noindent[\textbf{20:128}]
Does it not occur to them how many generations we have destroyed before them?--- they
walk in their very dwelling-places; verily, in that are signs to those endued with intelligence.

\noindent[\textbf{20:129}]
And had it not been for thy Lord's word already passed (the punishment) would have
been inevitable and (at) an appointed time.

\noindent[\textbf{20:130}]
Bear patiently then what they say, and celebrate the praises of thy Lord before the rising
of the sun, and before its setting, and at times in the night celebrate them; and at the ends of the
day; haply thou mayest please (Him).

\noindent[\textbf{20:131}]
And do not strain after what we have provided a few of them with --- the flourish of the
life of this world, to try them by; but the provision of thy Lord is better and more lasting.

\noindent[\textbf{20:132}]
Bid thy people prayer, and persevere in it; we do not ask thee to provide. We will
provide, and the issue shall be to piety.

\noindent[\textbf{20:133}]
They say, 'Unless he bring us a sign from his Lord --- What! has there not come to them
the manifest sign of what was in the pages of yore?'

\noindent[\textbf{20:134}]
But had we destroyed them with torment before it, they would have said, 'Unless Thou
hadst sent to us an apostle, that we might follow Thy signs before we were abased and put to
shame.'

\noindent[\textbf{20:135}]
Say, ' Each one has to wait, so wait ye but in the end ye shall know who are the fellows
of the level way, and who are guided!'

\section{Surah 21: Chapter of the Prophets (Mecca).}

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{21:1}]
Their reckoning draws nigh to men, yet in heedlessness they turn aside.

\noindent[\textbf{21:2}]
No reminder comes to them from their Lord of late, but they listen while they mock,

\noindent[\textbf{21:3}]
and their hearts make sport thereof! And those who do wrong discourse secretly (saying),
'Is this man aught but a mortal like yourselves? will ye accede to magic, while ye can see?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:4}]
Say, 'My Lord knows what is said in the heavens and the earth, He hears and knows!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:5}]
'Nay!' they say, 'a jumble of dreams; nay! he has forged it; nay! he is a poet; but let him
bring us a sign as those of yore were sent.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:6}]
No city before them which we destroyed believed --- how will they believe?

\noindent[\textbf{21:7}]
Nor did we send before them any but men whom we inspired? Ask ye the people of the
Scriptures if ye do not know.

\noindent[\textbf{21:8}]
Nor did we make them bodies not to eat food, nor were they immortal.

\noindent[\textbf{21:9}]
Yet we made our promise to them good, and we saved them and whom we pleased; but
we destroyed those who committed excesses.

\noindent[\textbf{21:10}]
We have sent down to you a book in which is a reminder for you; have ye then no sense?

\noindent[\textbf{21:11}]
How many a city which had done wrong have we broken up, and raised up after it
another people!

\noindent[\textbf{21:12}]
And when they perceived our violence they ran away from it.

\noindent[\textbf{21:13}]
'Run not away, but return to what ye delighted in, and to your dwellings! haply ye will be
questioned.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:14}]
Said they, 'O woe is us! verily, we were wrong-doers.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:15}]
And that ceased not to be their cry until we made them mown down,--- smouldering out!

\noindent[\textbf{21:16}]
We did not create the heaven and the earth and what is between the two in play.

\noindent[\textbf{21:17}]
Had we wished to take to a sport, we would have taken to one from before ourselves; had
we been bent on doing so.

\noindent[\textbf{21:18}]
Nay, we hurl the truth against falsehood and it crashes into it, and lo! it vanishes, but woe
to you for what ye attribute (to God)!

\noindent[\textbf{21:19}]
His are whosoever are in the heavens and the earth, and those who are with Him are not
too big with pride for His service, nor do they weary.

\noindent[\textbf{21:20}]
They celebrate His praises by night and day without intermission.

\noindent[\textbf{21:21}]
Or have they taken gods from the earth who can raise up (the dead)?

\noindent[\textbf{21:22}]
Were there in both (heaven and earth) gods beside God, both would surely have been
corrupted. Celebrated then be the praise of God, the Lord of the throne, above what they ascribe!

\noindent[\textbf{21:23}]
He shall not be questioned concerning what He does, but they shall be questioned.

\noindent[\textbf{21:24}]
Have they taken gods beside Him? Say, 'Bring your proofs. This is the reminder of those
who are with me, and of those who were before me.' Nay, most of them know not the truth, and
they do turn aside.

\noindent[\textbf{21:25}]
We have not sent any prophet before thee, but we inspired him that, 'There is no god but
Me, so serve ye Me.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:26}]
And they say, 'The Merciful has taken a son; celebrated be His praise!' --- Nay, honoured
servants;

\noindent[\textbf{21:27}]
they do not speak until He speaks; but at His bidding do they act.

\noindent[\textbf{21:28}]
He knows what is before them, and what is behind them, and they shall not intercede
except for him whom He is pleased with; and they shrink through fear.

\noindent[\textbf{21:29}]
And whoso of them should say, 'Verily, I am god instead of Him,' such a one we
recompense with hell; thus do we recompense the wrongdoers.

\noindent[\textbf{21:30}]
Do not those who misbelieve see that the heavens and the earth were both solid, and we
burst them asunder; and we made from water every living thing --- will they then not believe?

\noindent[\textbf{21:31}]
And we placed on the earth firm mountains lest it should move with them, and He made
therein open roads for paths, haply they may be guided!

\noindent[\textbf{21:32}]
and we made the heaven a guarded roof; yet from our signs they turn aside!

\noindent[\textbf{21:33}]
He it is who created the night and the day, and the sun and the moon, each floating in a
sky.

\noindent[\textbf{21:34}]
We never made for any mortal before thee immortality; what, if thou shouldst die, will
they live on for aye?

\noindent[\textbf{21:35}]
Every soul shall taste of death! we will test them with evil and with good, as a trial; and
unto us shall they return!

\noindent[\textbf{21:36}]
And when those who misbelieve see thee, they only take thee for a jest, 'Is this he who
mentions your gods?' Yet they at the mention of the Merciful do disbelieve.

\noindent[\textbf{21:37}]
Man is created out of haste. I will show, you my signs; but do not hurry Me.

\noindent[\textbf{21:38}]
And they say, 'When will this threat (come to pass), if ye tell the truth?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:39}]
Did those who misbelieve but know when the fire shall not be warded off from their faces
nor from their backs, and they shall not be helped!

\noindent[\textbf{21:40}]
Nay, it shall come on them suddenly, and shall dumbfounder them, and they shall not be
able to repel it, nor shall they be respited.

\noindent[\textbf{21:41}]
Prophets before thee have been mocked at, but that whereat they jested encompassed
those who mocked.

\noindent[\textbf{21:42}]
Say, 'Who shall guard you by night and by day from the Merciful?' Nay, but they from
the mention of their Lord do turn aside.

\noindent[\textbf{21:43}]
Have they gods to defend them against us? These cannot help themselves, nor shall they
be abetted against us.

\noindent[\textbf{21:44}]
Nay, but we have granted enjoyment to these men and to their fathers whilst life was
prolonged. Do they not see that we come to the land and shorten its borders? Shall they then
prevail?

\noindent[\textbf{21:45}]
Say, 'I only warn you by inspiration;' but the deaf hear not the call when they are
warned.

\noindent[\textbf{21:46}]
But if a blast of the torment of thy Lord touches them, they will surely say, 'O, woe is us!
verily, we were wrong-doers!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:47}]
We will place just balances upon the resurrection day, and no soul shall be wronged at
all, even though it be the weight of a grain of mustard seed, we will bring it; for we are good
enough at reckoning up.

\noindent[\textbf{21:48}]
We did give to Moses and Aaron the Discrimination, and a light and a reminder to those
who fear;

\noindent[\textbf{21:49}]
who are afraid of their Lord in secret; and who at the Hour do shrink.

\noindent[\textbf{21:50}]
This is a blessed reminder which we have sent down, will ye then deny it?

\noindent[\textbf{21:51}]
And we gave Abraham a right direction before; for about him we knew.

\noindent[\textbf{21:52}]
When he said to his father and to his people, 'What are these images to which ye pay
devotion?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:53}]
Said they, 'We found our fathers serving them.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:54}]
Said he, 'Both you and your fathers have been in obvious error.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:55}]
They said, 'Dost thou come to us with the truth, or art thou but of those who play?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:56}]
He said, 'Nay, but your Lord is Lord of the heavens and the earth, which He originated;
and I am of those who testify to this;

\noindent[\textbf{21:57}]
and, by God! I will plot against your idols after ye have turned and shown me your
backs!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:58}]
So he brake them all in pieces, except a large one they had; that haply they might refer it
to that.

\noindent[\textbf{21:59}]
Said they, 'Who has done this with our gods? verily, he is of the wrong-doers!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:60}]
They said, 'We heard a youth mention them who is called Abraham.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:61}]
Said they, 'Then bring him before the eyes of men; haply they will bear witness.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:62}]
Said they, 'Was it thou who did this to our gods, O Abraham?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:63}]
Said he, 'Nay, it was this largest of them; but ask them, if they can speak.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:64}]
Then they came to themselves and said, 'Verily, ye are the wrong-doers.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:65}]
Then they turned upside down again: 'Thou knewest that these cannot speak.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:66}]
Said he, 'Will ye then serve, beside God, what cannot profit you at all, nor harm you?

\noindent[\textbf{21:67}]
fie upon you, and what ye serve beside God! have ye then no sense?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:68}]
Said they, 'Burn him, and help your gods, if ye are going to do so!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:69}]
We said, 'O fire! be thou cool and a safety for Abraham!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:70}]
They desired to plot against him, but we made them the losers.

\noindent[\textbf{21:71}]
And we brought him and Lot safely to the land which we have blessed for the world,

\noindent[\textbf{21:72}]
and we bestowed upon him Isaac and Jacob as a fresh gift, and each of them we made
righteous persons;

\noindent[\textbf{21:73}]
and we made them high priests to guide (men) by our bidding, and we inspired them to
do good works, and to be steadfast in prayer, and to give alms; and they did serve us.

\noindent[\textbf{21:74}]
And Lot, to him we gave judgment and knowledge, and we brought him safely out of the
city which had done vile acts; verily, they were a people who wrought abominations!

\noindent[\textbf{21:75}]
And we made him enter into our mercy; verily, he was of the righteous!

\noindent[\textbf{21:76}]
And Noah, when he cried aforetime, and we answered him and saved him and his people
from the mighty trouble,

\noindent[\textbf{21:77}]
and we helped him against the people who said our signs were lies; verily, they were a
bad people, so we drowned them all together.

\noindent[\textbf{21:78}]
And David and Solomon, when they gave judgment concerning the field, when some
people's sheep had strayed therein at night; and we testified to their judgment;

\noindent[\textbf{21:79}]
and this we gave Solomon to understand. To each of them we gave judgment and
knowledge; and to David we subjected the mountains to celebrate our praises, and the birds
too,--- it was we who did it.

\noindent[\textbf{21:80}]
And we taught him the art of making coats of mail for you, to shield you from each
other's violence; are ye then grateful?

\noindent[\textbf{21:81}]
And to Solomon (we subjected) the wind blowing stormily, to run on at his bidding to the
land which Ave have blessed, --- for all things did we know,---

\noindent[\textbf{21:82}]
and some devils to dive for him, and to do other works beside that; and we kept guard
over them.

\noindent[\textbf{21:83}]
And Job, when he cried to his Lord, 'As for me, harm has touched me, but Thou art the
most merciful of the merciful ones.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:84}]
And we answered him, and removed from him the distress that was upon him; and we
gave his family, and the like of them with them, as a mercy from us, and a remembrance to those
who serve us.

\noindent[\textbf{21:85}]
And Ishmael, and Idris, and Dhu'l Kifl, all of these were of the patient:

\noindent[\textbf{21:86}]
and we made them enter into our mercy; verily, they were among the righteous.

\noindent[\textbf{21:87}]
And Dhu 'nnun, when he went away in wrath and thought that we had no power over
him; and he cried out in the darkness, ' There is no god but Thou, celebrated be Thy praise!
Verily, I was of the evildoers!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:88}]
And we answered him, and saved him from the trouble. Thus do we save believers!

\noindent[\textbf{21:89}]
And Zachariah, when he cried unto his Lord, 'O Lord! leave me not alone; for thou art
the best of heirs.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:90}]
And we answered him, and bestowed upon him John; and we made his wife right for
him; verily, these vied in good works, and called on us with longing and dread, and were humble
before us.

\noindent[\textbf{21:91}]
And she who guarded her private parts, and we breathed into her of our Spirit, and we
made her and her son a sign unto the worlds.

\noindent[\textbf{21:92}]
Verily, this your nation is one nation; and I am your Lord, so serve me.

\noindent[\textbf{21:93}]
But they cut up their affair amongst themselves; they all shall return to us;

\noindent[\textbf{21:94}]
and he who acts aright, and he who is a believer, there is no denial of his efforts, for,
verily, we will write them down for him.

\noindent[\textbf{21:95}]
There is a ban upon a city which we have destroyed that they shall not return,

\noindent[\textbf{21:96}]
until Yagug and Magug are let out, and they from every hummock shall glide forth.

\noindent[\textbf{21:97}]
And the true promise draws nigh, and lo! they are staring --- the eyes of those who
misbelieve! O, woe is us! we were heedless of this, nay, we were wrong-doers!

\noindent[\textbf{21:98}]
Verily, ye, and what ye serve beside God, shall be the pebbles of hell, to it shall ye go
down!

\noindent[\textbf{21:99}]
Had these been God's they would not have gone down thereto: but all shall dwell therein
for aye;

\noindent[\textbf{21:100}]
for them therein is groaning, but they therein shall not be heard.

\noindent[\textbf{21:101}]
Verily, those for whom the good (reward) from us was fore-ordained, they from it shall
be kept far away;

\noindent[\textbf{21:102}]
they shall not hear the slightest sound thereof, and they in what their souls desire shall
dwell for aye.

\noindent[\textbf{21:103}]
The greatest terror shall not grieve them; and the angels shall meet them, (saying), 'This
is your day which ye were promised!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:104}]
The day when we will roll up the heavens as es-Sigill rolls up the books; as we produced
it at its first creation will we bring it back again --- a promise binding upon us; verily, we are
going to do it.

\noindent[\textbf{21:105}]
And already have we written in the Psalms after the reminder that the earth shall my
righteous servants inherit.'

\noindent[\textbf{21:106}]
Verily, in this is preaching for a people who serve me!

\noindent[\textbf{21:107}]
We have only sent thee as a mercy to the worlds.

\noindent[\textbf{21:108}]
Say, 'I am only inspired that your God is one God; are ye then resigned?'

\noindent[\textbf{21:109}]
But if they turn their backs say, 'I have proclaimed (war) against all alike, but I know not
if what ye are threatened with be near or far!'

\noindent[\textbf{21:110}]
Verily, He knows what is spoken openly, and He knows what ye hide.

\noindent[\textbf{21:111}]
I know not, haply, it is a trial for you and a provision for a season.

\noindent[\textbf{21:112}]
Say, 'My Lord! judge thou with truth! and our Lord is the Merciful whom we ask for aid
against what they ascribe!'

\section{Surah 26: The Chapter of the Poets (Mecca).}
\vspace{-1em}(selection)


\noindent[\textbf{26:141}]
Thamud called the apostles liars;

\noindent[\textbf{26:142}]
when their brother Zalih said to them, 'Do ye not fear?

\noindent[\textbf{26:143}]
verily, I am to you a faithful apostle;

\noindent[\textbf{26:144}]
so fear God and obey me.

\noindent[\textbf{26:145}]
I do not ask you for it any hire; my hire is only with the Lord of the worlds.

\noindent[\textbf{26:146}]
Shall ye be left here in safety

\noindent[\textbf{26:147}]
with gardens and springs,

\noindent[\textbf{26:148}]
and corn-fields and palms,

\noindent[\textbf{26:149}]
the spathes whereof are fine? and ye hew out of the mountains houses skillfully.

\noindent[\textbf{26:150}]
But fear God and obey me;

\noindent[\textbf{26:151}]
and obey not the bidding of the extravagant,

\noindent[\textbf{26:152}]
who do evil in the earth and do not act aright!'

\noindent[\textbf{26:153}]
They said, 'Thou art only of the infatuated;

\noindent[\textbf{26:154}]
thou art but mortal like ourselves; so bring us a sign, if thou be of those who speak the
truth!'

\noindent[\textbf{26:155}]
He said, 'This she---camel shall have her drink and you your drink on a certain day;

\noindent[\textbf{26:156}]
but touch her not with evil, or there will seize you the torment of a mighty day!'

\noindent[\textbf{26:157}]
But they hamstrung her, and on the morrow they repented;

\noindent[\textbf{26:158}]
and the torment seized them; verily, in that is a sign; but most of them will never be
believers:

\noindent[\textbf{26:159}]
but verily, thy Lord He is mighty, merciful.

\section{Surah 43: The Chapter of Gilding (Mecca).}

IN the name of the merciful and compassionate God.

\noindent[\textbf{43:1}]
HA MIM.

\noindent[\textbf{43:2}]
By the perspicuous Book,

\noindent[\textbf{43:3}]
verily, we have made it an Arabic Qur'an; haply ye will have some sense.

\noindent[\textbf{43:4}]
And it is in the Mother of the Book with us,--- high and wise.

\noindent[\textbf{43:5}]
Shall we then push aside from you the Reminder, because ye are a people who are
extravagant?

\noindent[\textbf{43:6}]
How many prophets have we sent amongst those of yore?

\noindent[\textbf{43:7}]
and there never came to them a prophet but they did mock at him;

\noindent[\textbf{43:8}]
then we destroyed them --- more valiant than these; and the example of those of yore
passed away.

\noindent[\textbf{43:9}]
And if thou shouldst ask them who created the heavens and the earth, they will surely
say, 'The mighty, the knowing One created them,'

\noindent[\textbf{43:10}]
who made for you the earth a couch and placed for you therein roads, haply ye may be
guided:

\noindent[\textbf{43:11}]
and who sent down from the heaven water in due measure; and we raised up thereby a
dead country; thus shall ye too be brought forth;

\noindent[\textbf{43:12}]
and who has created all species; and has made for you the ships and the cattle whereon to
ride

\noindent[\textbf{43:13}]
that ye may settle yourselves on their backs; then remember the favour of your Lord
when ye settled thereon, and say, 'Celebrated be the praises of Him who hath subjected this to
us! We could not have got this ourselves;

\noindent[\textbf{43:14}]
and, verily, unto our Lord shall we return!'

\noindent[\textbf{43:15}]
Yet they make for Him of His servants offspring; verily, man is surely obviously
ungrateful.

\noindent[\textbf{43:16}]
Has He taken of what He creates daughters, and chosen sons for you?

\noindent[\textbf{43:17}]
Yet when the tidings are given any one of that which he strikes out as a similitude for the
Merciful One, his face grows black and he is choked.

\noindent[\textbf{43:18}]
What! one brought up amongst ornaments, and who is always in contention without
obvious cause?

\noindent[\textbf{43:19}]
And have they made the angels, who are the servants of the Merciful One, females? Were
they witnesses of their creation? their witness shall be written down, and they shall be
questioned;

\noindent[\textbf{43:20}]
and they say, 'Had the Merciful pleased we should never have worshipped them.' They
have no knowledge of that, they only conjecture.

\noindent[\textbf{43:21}]
Have we given them a book before it to which they might hold?

\noindent[\textbf{43:22}]
Nay; they say, 'We found our fathers (agreed) upon a religion, and, verily, we are guided
by their traces.'

\noindent[\textbf{43:23}]
Thus, too, did we never send before thee to a city any warner, but the affluent ones
thereof said, 'Verily, we found our fathers (agreed) upon a religion, and, verily, we are led by
their traces.'

\noindent[\textbf{43:24}]
Say, 'What! if I come to you with what is a better guide than what ye found your fathers
agreed upon?' and they will say, 'Verily, we, in what ye are sent with disbelieve!'

\noindent[\textbf{43:25}]
Then we took vengeance on them, and see how was the end of those who called the
(apostles) liars.

\noindent[\textbf{43:26}]
When Abraham said to his father and his people, 'Verily, I am clear of all that ye serve,

\noindent[\textbf{43:27}]
except Him who created me; for, verily, He will guide me:'

\noindent[\textbf{43:28}]
and he made it a word remaining among his posterity, that haply they might return.

\noindent[\textbf{43:29}]
Nay; but I let these (Meccans) and their fathers have enjoyment until the truth came to
them, and an apostle.

\noindent[\textbf{43:30}]
And when the truth came to them they said, 'This is magic, and we therein do
disbelieve!'

\noindent[\textbf{43:31}]
And they say, 'Unless this Qur'an were sent down to a man great in the two cities. . . . .'

\noindent[\textbf{43:32}]
Is it they who distribute the mercy of thy Lord? We distribute amongst them their
